Masked because both terminals set `COLORTERM`; it surfaced the moment F416's harness fix propagated `TERM` without it, dropping the container to 4-bit and taking every continuous colormap with it. **Open** — the fix is a shared terminal table, which is a C02 ruling. **Ruled and closed for the identification** — C02 I11 and commitment 14: one `identifyTerminal`, read by all three, asserted structurally because three lists that agree pass every agreement test. It found two things the entry missed: *latent* was wrong (**`ssh` forwards `TERM` and not `COLORTERM`**, so the case is live and not ours), and `TERM=xterm-ghostty` alone answered `imageProtocol: kitty` with `synchronisedUpdate: false` — **a disagreement a test asserted as correct** under a comment that is true about the code and false about Ghostty. The fourth instance is named and instrumented rather than ruled: identification is not capability, and only `mouse` asks whether a sequence reaches the emulator.

**F415** — `detectImageProtocol` returns `kitty` for `TERM=xterm-kitty` and nothing else, so the entire protocol arm was unreachable on Ghostty, WezTerm and Konsole — three of the four terminals the plan named. T1.7 asserts exactly the three cases the function implements, so rule and test agree and neither knows about a fourth terminal; and every other test injects capabilities directly, which is correct and leaves the detection as the one thing nothing exercises. **The knowledge was four lines up in the same table** — `synchronisedUpdate` has named ghostty since v1. **Fixed for ghostty on a measurement**; WezTerm and Konsole owed, with the probe named as their expiry.

**F454** — `writeDepth` compared a `double` against a `Float32Array` slot, so storing rounds and comparing does not: a second writer handing over the **identical** value passed `z < d.z[i]` whenever that rounding went up. **About half of all ties, decided by the last bits of a number nothing reads** — and *strictly nearer, so a tie is stable, so first-drawn wins* is the sentence three separate rules rest on, including the frame-drawn-last fix one finding along. **Nothing that compares depths can see it**: as doubles the two are equal, so an assertion that they tie passes. It showed as a missing marker glyph, and *which* marker went missing changed when an unrelated rounding convention changed. `Math.fround` on both sides is the whole fix. **The reusable shape**: a value stored in one precision and compared in another has an equality that is a coin flip, and a `Float32Array` beside a `double` is the ordinary way to write a depth buffer

**F453** — `strokeSeg` placed a sample with `Math.round` and every other writer in the same file uses `Math.floor`, so sample `i` covered `[i − 0.5, i + 0.5)` in one convention and `[i, i + 1)` in the other — **disagreeing for half of all coordinates**. A trajectory drifts off its own markers by up to a sample, systematically; and the rule that depends on the agreement (*points draw before lines, so a marker is never swallowed*) was decorative, because a tie needs both writers to be naming the same cell. **F445 one function over** — there the block origin was floored after subtracting half its width, and neither convention is visibly wrong alone

**F450** — three guards went into the 3D reference frame; **one had never worked, one no longer has a subject, and one never had one**, and the assertions written for all three agreed. `clipProject` moved an endpoint behind the eye to the near plane and `project` culls on `z <= NEAR` **inclusive**, so the clipped point was refused by the function the clip exists to satisfy and every segment it was written to save was dropped anyway. **Invisible for two independent reasons**: the clipped remainder is thrown outside `[0,1]²` at five of six distances swept, so a frame comparison agrees whether a segment is clipped, dropped, or clipped-then-dropped. **And the negative recorded in the spec was measured against the broken path** — *0 visible at every distance*, re-measured at **1 of 3 at `distance: 1.7`** once the clip worked: a probe run against a function that returns `null` measures the `null`. The label gap no longer reproduces `10.5` (24 azimuths, none abut) because three unrelated changes on the same commit moved the labels apart, and the anchor depth test fires nowhere because the anchors are pushed *outward*, toward the reader, by construction — both kept on asymmetry with their figures and the date. **The reusable part**: all three assertions were about a *consequence* — ink present, labels fewer, a run no longer than four characters — and a consequence is reachable by other mechanisms. **A guard whose only test is a consequence is one the next change can silence without failing anything**

**F449** — the 3D tick labels use the collision rule every other axis here uses — *drop the later, ordered by extent*, where collision means the cells **overlap** — and the frame read `10.5`. It is `1` and `0.5` at adjacent columns: two disjoint claims, both legal, both drawn, one token that is neither. **Two labels touching are unreadable without overlapping**, and an occupancy set reserving exactly the label's cells cannot say so. **Fixed** — the reservation is the label plus a blank each side, which is the spacing the y-gutter already has one dimension down and which nobody had written as a rule because there it falls out of the layout. **The axis names had it one level up**: placed past each positive end, and x and y both run *to* the anchor corner, so two names and a shared tick landed on one cell and the frame read `xy1` — they sit at their midpoints now. **The instrument is the frame and nothing else could be**: both are legal geometry, correct arithmetic, and a row every *is the label present* assertion agrees with

**F447** — `make all` is `check … e2e`, `check` type-checks the examples, the examples resolve `@fmx/calcium` to `dist/`, and **`dist/` is built by `e2e`** — the last target. So on any commit that widens a public type, `check` reads the *previous* build and passes. Measured with timestamps: `plot3d` joined `PlotForm`, check went green, `e2e` rebuilt `dist/` five minutes before the commit, and the example's `Record<PlotForm, Entry>` failed on the **next** commit's first check — **attributed to whatever landed in between**, which is the expensive half. **Not a stale cache but an ordering**: the examples are the only population that reads the *build*, and they are right to, because that is what a consumer does and R01 R4.4's reuse claim is exactly that. **Fixed** — `check` builds before the example loop, one `tsc -p tsconfig.build.json` that `e2e` pays again. **And the 24th forced declaration is the other half**: F441 counted 23 by running `tsc --noEmit` at the root, which is the root workspace only, so the count was right about what it measured and silent about a population it never reached — *a number derived from one command describes that command*

**F446** — the `plot3d` pass ran ten mutations and **two survived**, neither about the code. SC7 asserted `new Set(colours).size > 1` — *both clouds appear* — which a renderer with **no depth test at all** satisfies, because the loser still draws everywhere the winner is not. SC8 asserted `cs.size === 1` — *one reading, one colour* — which is true at the ramp's floor, at its top and at its middle. **One mechanism twice**, and both comments named the right property while both assertions counted: *containment is not correctness*, one level up, in rows written by someone holding that sentence. **Fixed by identifying**: the contested cell against the near cloud's own colour, and the zero span against `continuousColour(map, 0.5)` with the floor asserted to differ. **A fourth disposition for a survivor** — not *the code is wrong*, not *a second defect masks the first*, not *the spec sentence forbids nothing*, but **the assertion is about the wrong property and the comment above it names the right one**, which review cannot separate because the comment reads as the assertion

**F445** — the raster arm placed each point's `bw x bh` block at `floor(x) - (bw >> 1)`, half a sample **left and up** at every near point. **`make refdiff` found it**, not the suite: our helix sat two columns left of matplotlib's `scatter3D` with the same shape, and centring the block's origin took the external number 8.8% → 7.8%. **Three attempts at a row that catches it all failed for one reason**: a bounding box is unmoved by a sub-sample shift, and every cloud symmetric enough to assert against has a degenerate depth span, so every point lands in the middle tier where `bw` is 1 and the two placements are arithmetically identical. The row was **removed** rather than shipped passing both ways, and the mutation is recorded in the run file as one that kills nothing, with the reason. **The class lives where an independent renderer of the same geometry is the only reader** — invisible to arithmetic, to a stripped frame, and to a golden frame recorded after the defect existed

**F444** — `legendPlacement`'s count read `(segments?.length ?? 0) || series.length` and `identityOf`, in another file, read *segments, or series if it has none*. **The same sentence twice**, agreeing byte for byte on every form, because there were exactly two carriers and both clauses enumerate them. A **third** carrier is what separates them: `plot3d` holds its identities in `points3` and only under `colourBy: "series"`, so `identityOf` learned a third arm and the copy could not — leaving a legend whose *presence* and *contents* rules sit in two places with nothing to keep them in step. The failure would have been a key that never draws while the labels exist. **The signal was a correct fix changing nothing observable**; the question that reaches it is *which callers do not call the function*, and the answer was the one resolver whose whole job is the same question. **A reimplemented rule keeps its birthday clauses**, in its cheapest form. **Fixed** — one call, behaviour-identical on all forty-four existing forms, which is what made it invisible

**F442** — the 3D raster's first draft used the **colour** as the drawn flag: `ink[i] === undefined` meaning *nothing there*. `continuousColour` returns `undefined` below its own floor, so a sample the depth buffer had accepted would compose as blank — a point silently deleted, block the right height, every geometry assertion passing. **It is unreachable today and that is the finding rather than the reprieve**: `halfBlockEligible` requires `colourDepth >= 8` and `CONTINUOUS_FLOOR` is 8. Two thresholds in two components, written by two different arguments — how many colours a cell needs to carry a picture, and when a perceptual ramp stops being one — that happen to be the same number, with no reason to consult each other. Either moving alone turns a drawn point into a blank. **Fixed** — the depth buffer is the source of drawn-ness and the colour is what it is drawn in, `undefined` included; the glyph arm was already right because its table index and its colour are separate arrays

**F439** — PR8 exists to refuse a scratch buffer and its first draft asserted two **proxies**: `b.z !== a.z` and `b` cleared to `Infinity`. A scratch array handed out as `SCRATCH.subarray(0, n)` passes both — `subarray` returns a fresh view object per call, and the `fill` clears the view. **Two green assertions over one array.** The property a shared buffer cannot have is that an *earlier* render survives a later one, which is what the row asserts now. **Found by the mutation pass and by nothing else** — the row passed and the thing it exists to refuse walked through it. **And the rule that would have caught it cannot be widened**: SS24's `why` says *binding* and its pattern says `let|var`, so a `const` bound to a mutable is invisible — measured at one instance in the whole scope, `DIM_FACTORS`, which is a pure memo over frozen data. Widening would fire on the legitimate case and leave an exemption reading as permission. Blind spot recorded beside the rule with the figure

**F437** — SP1 decided a citation's owner by **position**: `GROUP` took every paren group that did not *open* with `→` and read every `I\d+` inside as local, so an arrow in the *middle* sent a cross-reference to the local arm. **Wrong in both directions and the direction that shipped was the false pass** — 8 mixed groups in commitments, 8 foreign tokens, **8 of 8 silent because the citing spec declared the same number**, and a false failure the first time one did not. A second defect in the same arm read `/^(I\d+[a-z]?)/` off the start of a multi-token target, dropping 10 of 20 tokens across 10 groups. **The existing fixture was true and tested one of the two ways to write a cross-reference** — an arrow-opening group — which is F84's class inside a test. Re-measured **through the repaired rule** rather than through a hand parser: all 8 perturbed, all 8 reported against their own target, corpus clean. The repair's own regression — `(→ A02 §1)` becoming *cites nothing* on 8 commitments — has its own row. Blind spot stated and demonstrated: an arrow-less `(C11 I17, I9)` stays local, because the arrow is the mark and guessing would be wrong in the other direction. **Fixed**, 6/6 mutations with a control

| | the gate | what it was not checking |
|---|---|---|
| **F408** | **every row in `refresh.test.ts`, for a part that is not at the top level** | every live part any real surface nests — `/faults`, `/monitor`, S13's dashboard | `currentPanel` read the block back with `doc.blocks.find(…)`, **top level only**, while `liveDeclarations` recurses and says why in its own comment: *S13's dashboard is five panels inside a `group`, so a walk over top-level blocks alone finds none of them — which would be a dashboard that renders its placeholders and never ticks.* **One question a step apart, and the recursion was added on one side.** Writes were fine — a patch addresses by id and the transcript resolves through the tree — so a nested part declared, rendered, fetched and patched, and every sweep that must **read the block in place** found nothing: the elapsed counter (C23 I52, landed at F227 and never run in a real session since), the staleness re-title (I35), and the retry countdown. Every row here puts its panel at the top level and no real surface does. **Fixed** — the read walks on C04's `hasChildren`; `T3.55c` nests a part and failed before it. **Fixed** |
| **F407** | **the sweep, for anything that is not a *loading* box** | every failing live part in every app | `retryInMs` was written once, at the failure, so `retrying in 12s` stood still for the whole backoff and jumped — measured, seventeen frames with the spinner advancing and the number fixed. Every ingredient was present: the driver knows `dueAt`, the sweep runs, the field is a field, and the only arm asked for `loading`. **The bit that made a safe fix possible was being spent**: `partOf` resolved `renderError`'s default at declaration, which is the resolution `renderLoading` beside it deliberately avoids, so the driver could not tell its own block from a consumer's. `renderError` is `| null` now and the fallback moved to `refresh.ts` — **the shape C24 §5 already called tidier and did not take**. The attempt counter was **correct**: 1→3s, 2→6s, 3→12s, 4→24s, doubling exactly. And the first row written for it asserted the write and not the **arming**, which the harness satisfies either way. **Fixed** |
| **F404** | **the collision sweep's own note, for a member the explanation did not cover** | I75 grouped `violin/braille` with `violin/braille-filled` — a pair differing in **exactly** `plotFill` — and the note beside the group explained all four members as *the terminal's choices about how to spend a cell … a resolution fact rather than a dropped field*. Three of the four are. **A pixel curve fills or strokes at any resolution**, and `densityFigure` emitted `fill: true` unconditionally, so the member crossed nowhere. **An explanation that fits most of a set absorbs the member it does not**, and it survives review because a reader checks it against the examples in front of them. I80 had it filed as *blocked until `violin` draws* and the family had drawn since F383, so it sat on two lists and neither said owed. **Fixed** — fills on `plotFill === "solid"`, the terminal's own test; C04 I59 refuses `solid` on the line arm, so the arms now agree on the absent case too. 16 SVG frames moved, one attribute each; the terminal baseline did not. Distinct documents 149 → **152**. **Fixed** |
| **F403** | **`G1c`, for whether it still stands for its own subject** | §3ak.37 says the violin refusal expires *the day `violinRows` stops taking a width*. It does still take one — the terminal arm is per-cell — and the refusal went anyway, through a shared fixed-resolution curve. This arc's spec commit concluded the guard was therefore **stale and standing for nothing**, from its doc comment. **Reading the assertions**: the row was inverted *with* the fix, keeps the arity as a **control**, and adds the claim that matters — the same point count at two output widths. **A row's doc comment describes it as written**, and an inverted row keeps the paragraph that motivated its control, so *the comment says arity* and *the row asserts arity* are both true of a row whose subject has moved. Corrected one commit late. **Fixed** |
| **F401a** | **`roadmap-status`, for whether a cited line holds what the row says** | Entry 10 cited `ctx.ask` at `src/shell/execution.ts:616`; it is at **806** and had been for some time. The verifier asserts the line is **non-blank**, and 616 held something — so a citation wrong by 190 lines passed every run. **RS2c's own recorded limit arriving as an instance**, and within one file rather than across two. Exposed by adding one `import` far above, which is the only thing that could: the citation resolves against no symbol, so nothing but a shift onto whitespace moves it. Corrected. **Not closed as a class** — matching *"`ctx.ask` at line N"* against the identifier at line N is the citation-resolves-against-the-wrong-thing problem `COMMITMENT_INVARIANT_AUDIT.md` argues against automating. **Fixed** |
| **F400** | **the plots suite, for the document its 53 rungs land in** | `/all` and `/form <name>` drew **nothing at all** for a session while thirteen rows were green, one of them asserting **95 figures by equality**. F396 gave every form its rungs and every rung kept the form's own `` `f-${form}` `` id, so `/all` held twenty `f-line` and `/form violin` twelve `f-violin`; C04 I14 refuses the **whole document**, above every block that could have reported it. Every assertion was about a **piece** — each rung built alone, compared to its default, counted — and the composition lived in `main.ts`, which calls `tui.start()` at module scope, so **the one artefact that could have failed was the one no test could construct**. Fourth instance of the duplicate-id class here (F372, F373, F399). Fixed by a seam: the *table* still cannot name an id, the *composer* must. `main.ts` is wiring only; every document is built in `src/commands.ts` and `T-doc2` runs all 97 through `expectDocument().isValid()`, with `T-doc1` gating the coverage table against the manifest by equality. **Two fabrications corrected the gate itself**: the fixture invented a `meta` the shell publishes (`completeLocal`), and the live-part row validated a rendered child *alone*, where a child collides with nothing — widened to the host's blocks plus the child, all three historical instances fail it. **Found by a reader typing two commands.** **Fixed** |
| **F381** | **every gate, for the chunked transmission's alignment** | `chunked()` reserves 4042 payload bytes an escape — **not a multiple of four** — and kitty decodes base64 **per chunk**, so every continuation is misaligned. One chunk drew, two drew **nothing**, measured by bisecting the raster in a real kitty. The escape is well-formed under every reading available here: nine chunks, options on the first, `m=1` through `m=0`, right box. **The header reasoned its way to the right doubt and stopped a step short** — it names the 4096 cap as a protocol claim *not measurable here*, and alignment is a second claim in the same class that nothing named. **Three defects in one arm, each hiding the next**: F379's bound meant it never ran, so F380's `c=1` was invisible; fixing the box let it run, so this became visible — and only at a raster needing two escapes. **Fixed** |
| **F379** | **every gate, for an arm bounded at forty cells** | `MAX_PLACEHOLDER_SPAN` was the diacritic table's length and the table shipped as a **40-entry prefix of kitty's 297**, so `placementRows` refused every image wider than forty cells and the renderer fell back to the dither — deliberately, correctly, and on everything. **The arm had never drawn a pixel** after a walk, an invariant, a validator arm, a renderer, three probes and IK2's assertions, all of which pass: IK2 checks the escape's *form*, and a refusal that falls back is well-formed. **The justifying sentence is true about the wrong axis** — *forty entries cover forty rows and forty columns, which is past any height a transcript block declares* — right about height, silent about width, and a block's width is the terminal's. MG24's shape (F84). Found by running it in a real kitty, which is what C09 §4c names and nothing had done. **Fixed** — all 297, from kitty's own table, not guessed: combining class 230 gives 510 in a different order |
| **F380** | **`image-kitty.test.ts`, for the parameter that sizes the placement** | With the table fixed the arm is taken and **nothing draws**. One APC emitted, `c=1,r=14`, against **784 placeholders spanning 56 columns** — the transmission declared a one-cell-wide image, from a literal `1` at three call sites. **The comment above them is wrong twice and reads as care**: *the renderer derives the same numbers from the same block* (it derives them from the block **and the width**, so there was one computation and one constant, not two) and *`c` and `r` are advisory to kitty* (a claim with no record — `c` sizes the virtual placement). Every existing assertion passes on the old code, because `c=1` is a well-formed escape. **Fixed** — the frame's width threaded through, the box from `imageCells` |
| **F373** | **`validateDocument`, on the document a patch produces** | `validate.ts` says *I2, I3, I14 and I27 are established here and nowhere else*, and the transcript's `patch` commits `applyPatch`'s result without revalidating. Measured: patch #1 **succeeds** and yields a document `validateDocument` refuses — `id "walk" appears 2 times (C04 I14)` — and patch #2 is then rejected with the same invariant's message. **The defect is detected one tick late**, at the point where nothing can attribute it: the patch that created the invalid state passed, and the one that merely met it failed. Reachable from the published builder with nothing warning, because a live panel and the block it renders taking one name is the obvious thing to write |
| **F369** | **the reader map every ruling in this arc opened with** | *How many times does each member appear in `svg.ts`* — eleven came back `svg=0` and it was read as *the arm does not read it*. **It measures which members the file names.** A member reaching the arm through a shared `Plot → Plot` transform is never written there, and `calendarUnit` and `startDate` are exactly that: `svg.ts` calls `drawnBlock`, which routes `calendar` to `calendarRows`, **moved out of a terminal renderer by F322**. Measured by rendering instead of grepping: five blocks differing only in these two draw **five distinct documents**. Both crossed a year ago and the arc counted them owed for six commits. The remainder is **two**, not four. **F355's member sweep shares the blind spot** — it reads the type against the files — and a grep over names cannot see a value arriving through a transform, which is what this whole pass has been building |
| **F370** | **the two fixtures, and the order they were run in** | `xTitle` and `axisCross` are the genuinely owed two, and neither is isolated by the corpus: `line/x-title` sets `xLabels` beside the title, `line/axis-cross` sets `xMin`/`xMax` beside the crossing. Two variants added, each named for what its block sets — `x-captions` (three captions, no title) and `straddle-zero` (both axes straddle zero, rules at the edge) — **and the collision sweep run on them before either member was touched**, which is what caught `compact-box-solid` earlier in this arc. Two collisions reported by the instrument rather than by inspection; four distinct terminal frames |
| **F368** | **`legendOf`, for every block whose callout names its series** | `yCallout: "name"` and `"both"` write the identity at the line's end, and `legendPlacement` has removed the legend for them since §3ag — *a name at the line's end **is** the legend*. **`legendOf`, the crossed resolver, never carried that clause**, so this arm drew `alpha` and `beta` in a legend the terminal removes, identically for all three callout variants: the collision group F349 already reported and nobody had read. **The second half is a wrong picture, not an absence** — it would have drawn the legend *beside* the callouts once they landed, the identity three times. Closed by moving the clause and crossing the member: **the strings** are `calloutOf`'s, **the rung** stays in the terminal (below the colour floor the family stacks into labelled strips, so a name there is a third copy), and **the anchor** is each arm's own — a cell row and a pixel y, the same question in two units. 1840 terminal frames, 0 moved |
| **F367** | **my own AD13 comment, and two of the four remaining rulings** | *Three cheap ones, because the sweep already reports them* — and one of the three was wrong in my own note inside AD13. **`calendar/day` and `day-stretch` differ in `matrixAnchor`, not `calendarUnit`**: both set `calendarUnit: "day"`, so the member the sweep isolates is the anchor, and `calendarUnit`'s four variants draw different grids and never collide. **`matrixAnchor` is legitimate** — all four anchors give one document, every cell 7.487px, and both of its jobs are cell quanta: `stretch` against `uniform` distributes 53 columns over 74 cells (*a doubling at a pitch of one*, says its own doc) where 640/53 divides exactly, and `window` against `left` decides what to drop, which **F318 already ruled** — this arm scales and has nothing to drop. **I80's third instance**, composing with the other two rather than repeating them. **`yCallout` is owed**: the terminal writes `█ beta` on the row a series ends at, this arm draws a legend and its texts are identical across all three callout variants — direct labelling is not a cell idiom, and it simply does not read the member |
| **F366** | **`plotCorners`, and the two probes that read it backwards** | I80 asks *does the choice exist because of cells?* Corner cells against **width** are stable — 37 at 40, 60 and 80 — which reads as *tracks the data*. The second arm draws a `<path>` of fifty `L` segments, and `stroke-linejoin` is a corner vocabulary, which reads as *expressible, so owed*. **Against height it settles**: 15, 37, 61, 79 at heights 4, 8, 16, 24, for a curve with **three** turning points. Neither the keyword nor the name is the discriminator — the **line shape** is, and **40 published object types under `src/` are single-line**. F84's sibling: a rule widened along the axis one finding named can stay narrow along one it did not, and the earlier fix is what makes the clean result read as coverage. **Instance fixed** — both C26 types reformatted, with the reason recorded at each declaration; **class open** |
| **F160** | **MG24's member matching** | it compares members **by name**, so `ElementReport.kindsCovered` reported a *different* type's exemption stale. The harmless direction; the same looseness means **a genuinely unconsumed member is satisfied the moment any unrelated type anywhere declares a field with that name** — and `id`, `kind`, `width`, `rows` are exactly the names a new type carries. Nothing in a clean run tells the two apart. **Instance renamed**, blind spot recorded at the declaration; **class open** — the fix is keying by `(owner, name)` |
| **F252** | the framework — **an escape with a length limit, emitted whole.** kitty caps a direct transmission at 4096 bytes and continues with `m=1` until a final `m=0`; phase 1's `transmit` emitted one escape carrying the entire payload. **IK2's structural assertions all passed**, because the corpus fixture is an 8x8 PNG — seventy bytes of base64, legal at that size and illegal at any useful one — and **no in-repo test has a terminal**, so this is the plane-16 class. Found by building the composited arm, whose raw RGBA is *larger* than the PNG it replaces (7595 bytes against 1741): thinking about payload size raised the question and the question found the shipped defect. Taken on the asymmetry rather than on certainty — chunked is correct under both readings and unchunked draws **nothing**, blamed on the image. **And the reserve was off by one in the fix**: `- 8` against a 9-byte frame, so every first chunk came out at 4097. The row that caught it asserted **the bound** rather than the chunking — an assertion about the mechanism agrees with an off-by-one | **fixed** |
| **F256** | the framework — **the gate was written, found, run, and passed against the violation it names.** C12 §3aj is the best-placed gate here: written two phases early, put beside the geometry it governs, four hazards, five rows, its own preconditions. Both preconditions held — catalogue reproducible at 1780 files over two runs, `niceAxis` unit-free so the refactor was small as predicted — and the refactor passed at **0 of 377 golden rows and 0 of 1780 catalogue frames**. **Then the same gate passed hazard 1's own violation**: moving the flat-line answer into the normalised layer is wrong at *every even row count* (`floor(last/2)` against `round(0.5·last)`) and moved nothing. Counting the branch says why — **0 hits in either corpus**, because neither contains a constant series. **A03 §2's vacuity class arriving in a gate**, and in the good one: a gate phrased over a corpus inherits its blind spots without inheriting a way to see them. What separated the readings was a **fabricated violation** — the one instrument A03 asks for everywhere else and this gate did not have. Remedy is a companion rather than a rewording: rows that construct what the corpus does not, plus a pass that says they can fail. **And one row was removed rather than declared an expected survivor** — byte-identical and uncatchable, and a permanent survivor turns a pass's signal off for good | **fixed** (§3aj.1) |
| **F257** | the framework's own gates — **three commit messages cited a gitignored directory as evidence.** `docs/catalogue/` is in `.gitignore`, so `git status --porcelain docs/catalogue | wc -l` returns **0 whether a frame moved or not** — and it appears in three commits from one session as half of §3aj's evidence. **The same bit for *clean* and for *cannot see***, which is the class F256 was filed about, committed three times by the author of F256. **The conclusion survives on the other half**: `test/golden/` is tracked, 20 snapshots, 377 rows, and the flat-line branch count went through the renderer rather than through git. The catalogue sentence was decoration reading as corroboration. **It survived three writings because `git status <path>` looks like it checks that path**, and a blind check and a passing check are both an empty string. Remedy is a digest rather than a discipline — `tools/catalogue-hash.mjs`, 956 frames, reproducible. **Plus an ordering hazard found the same hour**: `plot-catalogue.mjs` clears the directory before writing, so running it second deletes the 66 phase frames — watched, 66 became 1 | **fixed** |

**F82 was found producing this file** and it is the group's sharpest instance, because the
gate that failed is the one verifying the sentence three sections down. `citations` was
incremented inside the violation branch, so it counted *failures* — a second name for
`violations.length`, reporting **0** against a tree holding hundreds. Zero is also what it
reports if the regex matches nothing, if the scope excludes every citing file, or if the walk
comes back empty: **the exact two failures SP5 had already shipped, and the field existed to
tell them apart.**

**Then the working counter found the next one within a minute.** `415` looked low against the
tree, and it was: **thirteen top-level documents under `examples/docker/` were outside the
scope entirely — 250 citations, of which this file holds 175.** The most-cited artefact after
the ledger, and the one whose whole job is to cite. That is the rule's *first* vacuity
repeating — a scope naming the directories thought to matter instead of covering the directory
and naming exceptions — so it is three instances of one class in one rule. All 250 resolve, so
nothing was hidden; what was missing was the guarantee, not a defect.

**Its test agreed, and the way it agreed is the part to keep.**

```ts
expect(v.citations, "it looked at the citation and accepted it").toBe(0);
```

The message states the intent; the number asserts the opposite. Both were written in one
sitting from one understanding and neither was checked against the other — **F65's shape, an
artefact wrong about itself, arriving in a test rather than in a drawing.** The corpus row
meanwhile asserted `scanned > 50`, which counts *files opened* and stays high through both
failure modes; only `citations` shows that the regex and the scope work **together**.

Fixed, and mutated: reverting the increment kills both new rows and neither `fires` row —
which is why four green rows and a dead field coexisted for as long as they did.

### The four open ones are about a rule's *reach*, and they fail in different directions

**F83 is a definition too weak inside its scope.** A02 Seam 4 is about a component complete on
its own side with nothing on the other — and the implementation is the *same* side. The file
boundary was taken as a proxy for the seam and within a component it is not one. 28 members
never cross their own component; `HistoryStore.rerun` has no caller in `src/` — a test
calls it, and that qualifier is the difference between a claim and a slogan — and
`TransportRouter.busy` **survived its own removal** — `router.ts:64` says a guard *"replaced
`busy`"* and `construct.ts:1024` counts *"seventeen until `busy`"*, so the tree records the
deletion twice and the member is still there.

**F84 is a scope too narrow with a definition that works.** Its present contents are almost
empty — 0 members dead outright, and the 67 that never cross a component are dominated by
deferrals already documented, `ToolDef.oneShot` having three paragraphs in C22 §4. Three
survive: `GlyphSet.teeLeft`, `teeRight` and `hollow`, declared glyph slots with no drawer.

**Filed anyway, and this is the group's argument in one line: a rule whose scope excludes
three-quarters of its subject is a rule whose clean result means much less than it reads,
whatever it happens to contain today.**

**F159 is the third, and it is the one that could only be found after F84 was fixed.** MG24
now walks type aliases — which is what closes F84's axis and what makes its result read as
covered. It still reads members off their own lines, so a declaration written on one line
presents none: forty published object types under `src/` are outside the rule by their
formatting alone, and nothing chose that. **A rule widened along the axis a finding named
can stay narrow along one it did not, and the earlier fix is precisely what hides the
remainder.** That is F82's sentence about a counter, applied to a
scope — and it is why this group's disposition is never "fix the instance".

**F60 is three findings stacked and only the third is new.** F36 and F37 are why the deep
imports exist; this is that the reach was aimed at a *repository*, so a workaround for a
missing export silently excluded itself from the check that exists to test the package.
**A workaround that cannot survive the boundary it works around is a second defect wearing
the first one's clothes.**

`tools/capture.py` now spawns the bin rather than the module, so every frame this repository
reads goes through the entry point a user has.

**F489** — adding a third rung turned up **two latent defects and one new one, all the same shape**: a variable meaning two things while the two happened to be equal. `grid.width` was the cell width *and* the sample width — `frameOf` places a label's row from `rows` (cells) and its column from `grid.width` (samples), and both existing arms made them equal, so the asymmetry sat in one function unremarked until a rung that is `width × 2` scattered every label to the margins. And `half ? undefined : densityGlyph(…)` fired on the braille rung, where the surface's second channel makes no sense, so every surface sample wrote a glyph the compose step then withheld from the dot grid. **The second is the instructive one because the picture was plausible** — a stipple, which is what a reader half-expecting braille to stipple would accept. What said otherwise was a histogram of the eight dot positions: **3 against 76** before, 59 to 61 after, with the wireframe's even row as the control. **A boolean naming one of two arms becomes a lie the moment there is a third, and does not become a compile error**; the remedy is a named union plus a second predicate for *paints a sub-cell sample*, and the audit was `grep "half\b"` over one file

**F490** — BR4's first draft measured a marker's **bounding box in cells** and bounded it between the half rung's box and one cell more, with a correct reason for the tolerance: the two rungs round a block's origin into different lattices. **The mutation pass killed it** — replacing the doubled tier table with the half rung's, quartering every marker's area, moved the bounding box by nothing the tolerance could see. **Because a mark has a floor of one cell**, and what quartering destroys is *sub-cell coverage*, which a box measured in cells is precisely the quantity that cannot see. The assertion that bites is a full cell: a near marker at the doubled table is `4 × 4` dots — one cell, all eight positions — and at the half table `2 × 2`, which cannot fill a cell at any alignment. **8 against 0**, with its own control that non-full cells exist. **The class: when the property is an area, do not assert an extent** — they agree on every input above the floor, which is every input a fixture picks, and the tolerance that survives lattice noise is the same one that makes the row blind
## 12 · A time-based assertion under contention — **4** · new at F80

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **F262** | **six rows whose verdict is a function of machine load** | `vitest.config.ts` sets no `testTimeout`, so 5000ms governs, and six rows parse or shell over the whole tree: the module graph, the commitment scan, the anchor sweep across 98 runs, the catalogue's SGR parser. **`MA4` is 1539ms alone and 5033–7345ms in the suite; `T6.5/T6.6` is 1723ms alone and 5114–5489ms.** Three consecutive runs, three different failing sets — five rows, then three, then two — every one passing alone. **The config already reasons about flakiness and this walked past it**: its comment is about a clock a test asks for, and the clock these race is one nobody chose. **It cost three red runs against a change that moved zero frames**, which is what a real regression looks like from outside. **Fixed** — `testTimeout: 30_000` with both figures beside it, a ceiling rather than six arguments because the rule is *a row that reads the whole tree needs room* and a per-row list stops seeing the seventh. Three runs after: green, green, green |

**Calcium's own suite, not the app's**, and it is here because a failure of this kind names an
enforcement rule and sends a reader looking for a violation that does not exist.

| | the victim shape |
|---|---|
| **F69** | a **threshold** — T5.3a asserts a background stream advanced by more than three while sixty keystrokes are processed. On a contended runner it advanced by one. Same commit, red on a branch and green on `main` four minutes later |
| **F73** | a **timeout** and a **ratio** — six tier-2 source scans timed out at 15 s having taken 24 s; C17 T3.15 read 6.0 against a limit of 3 on a 1 MB paste. **Read 4.05 again on 2026-08-15**, on a docs-only commit that touched no `src/`, green on an immediate re-run at load average 3.0 — a second measurement of the same row, and the one that makes *not an algorithmic regression* a fact rather than an inference |
| **F320** | **a fixture that types at a clock** | `examples/minimal`'s PTY harness sends `/list` at **1.5 s** and its Enter at 3.5 s; the package takes **3.35 / 3.79 / 4.35 s** to import `dist` and reach the prompt, measured three times. Both writes went into a terminal with no application listening, and the capture ends with `❯ /list` — the prompt drawn *after* the keystrokes — which reads exactly like a shell ignoring a command. **It passed on a quick machine and failed on a busy one**, so the row was asserting the host. **Three wrong hypotheses first, every one about the environment**: a half-written `dist` from a killed `make e2e` (a clean rebuild did not fix it), a stale symlink (it resolves correctly), and **1098 zombie processes** from the same killed run — real, reaped by restarting the container, and not the cause. A stash and a checkout to the previous commit failed there too, which cleared the day's work and left the harness. **The question that ended it was about the fixture rather than the environment** — *what is this row waiting for?* Nothing. **Fixed**: the command goes when the prompt appears, three green runs, and a control whose prompt never appears still captures the banner and no table. **Stated limit**: a 20 s outer deadline replaces the 9 s window, so a hung shell takes 20 s to fail |

**The ratio is the one worth naming**, because it fails in a way that reads as an algorithmic
regression rather than as a busy machine. So the class is not *slow rows time out*: it is
**anything whose assertion is about time.**

`make all` reproduces T5.6 with no load generator at all — three times for three, at 291–301 s
against 214–225 s for every subset, which is what says this is the machine rather than an
ordering defect.

**`make load-down` keeps its place on the asymmetry, not the odds**, and both measurements are
recorded: the step-8 re-measure did not reproduce, and F69's pair four minutes apart did. A row
that fails under contention will fail eventually whether or not anyone introduced it.

**F812** — the emulator gate green in the devcontainer and red on the runner: kitty answering Shift on every key, xterm with no window in eight seconds; neither reproduced on the runner's own distribution, so the fixture is hardened on every axis the failures touch and the next run is the measurement

## 13 · Text the framework emits — **6**

**F55**. `PROMPT = "❯ "` and `b.live`'s default `loading…` are constants concatenated into a
frame with nothing between them and the terminal. C09 §4 argues at length that a glyph is a
slot and never a character, *because* a block-supplied one was emitted verbatim and broke under
`LANG=C`. These are L4 text, outside that vocabulary, and they break the same way.

**Consumer count is the ranking here and it undercounts this one**: there is exactly one
prompt, it is on every frame the shell ever draws, it is on the line the reader types into, and
no application can replace it. It is low on the list because it wants the same ruling C09 §4
already made — folding it in is cheaper than making a second one.

**Keyed here on 2026-09-04, moved from the F142 cohort table under §14 where they had been counted against the wrong group** — the section keyed no id at all (`**F55.**` carried its full stop inside the bold, which SP6's reader does not match), so its ranking row said 6 over a section the reader saw as empty, and group 14 read 30 against 24.

| **F215** | the framework emits a sentence about a vocabulary one fork out of date — a rejected theme preference is *not dark or light* on a set that holds three, and no test asserts the string |
