## 14 · A constant, a channel or a rule that outlived its configuration — **24** · new at F492

**The mechanism, in one sentence: a decision that was correct for every configuration that
existed, kept after another one arrived.** Not a mistake at the time and not a mistake anyone
made later — the thing it depended on moved, and nothing watches a dependency that was never
written as one. Every member reads as deliberate, which is why none was found by review.

**F729** — the SVG arm's tick count is `ticksFor(plotAreaRows(block))` — derived from the block's declared **row** height — while the canvas height is whatever `svgLayout` was given, and the corpus only ever renders at 320. A rule configured for cells, running against a fixed pixel canvas. At `height: 40` that is **19 right-hand labels at 15.29 px pitch with a 12 px font — 3.29 px of leading** — and it saturates at 19, so nothing overprints and **the ceiling is the only reason**. Take the same block to a shorter canvas and the value labels overprint **each other**: 11.47 px at `svgLayout(640, 240)`, 9.55 at 200, 7.64 at 160, 5.73 at 120. **`RC1` cannot see it** — it indexes callout against label, so label-against-label is outside its shape however many frames it sweeps — which is F725's class with both writers being the axis emitter. Owed a row and a ruling: a width crossing the seam in the direction §3aj hazard 3 does not name. Open

**F704** — `SVG_EM = 0.6` was in the file documented as *this arm's own estimate*, and the request proposed building the reserve on it. Measured from `hmtx ÷ head.unitsPerEm` on the shipped `.ttf` files and confirmed by rendering 20 glyphs at size 120 through the same `sharp` the goldens go through: **0.6001** Courier New, Andale Mono, Monaco; **0.6021** DejaVu Sans Mono (the container's `fc-match monospace`) and Menlo; **0.6182** SF Mono. **Every measured face is above 0.6, in the one direction that clips.** The arm emits `font-family="monospace"` — a generic family, so the face is the renderer's choice and the metric is **unknowable at emit time** — and the constant is used only for reserving room and for deciding how many characters fit in room, both of which want an **upper bound**. `SVG_EM_MAX = 0.65` is 5% above the widest measured, costs 4.6 px of blank on the corpus's widest string, and **states its own falsification: a face above 0.65**. F703 is the 13 strings it was clipping and F705 the head it was cutting sub-pixel

**F652** — `categorical.carries === "decoration"` on every shipped theme, so C10 I15 exempts its eight slots from contrast validation and lint-restricts them to *declared art* — and `svg.ts:1158` paints a line's end-of-series callout in `categorical.cN` as a `<text>` fill on the page, under F382's ruling that both arms agree with the legend. So eight slots are checked against **nothing, on any surface**, while carrying the only text that names a series. §4's own conditional one axis over: a palette exempted because it carries no meaning, and a surface that paints it as meaning. The figures are comfortable — worst 6.26 dark, 5.17 light, 7.55 high-contrast — and T2.27 is now the only row that asks, because it reads fills off the document rather than palettes off the theme

**F661** — SS39 — *a block names a glyph slot* — carries the fourteen `Glyph` token names as a **literal alternation**, so adding `quote` and `nested` to the union, to `GLYPH_TABLE` and to `GLYPH_MEMBERS` (both `satisfies Record<Glyph, true>`, so the compiler holds two of the three copies) made the rule report the new tokens as defects. The vocabulary is declared in three places of which two are checked and one is a string; both copies read as correct and the one that goes stale is the one nothing asserts against. Extended by hand with a note; the class fix is to derive the list from `GLYPH_TOKENS`, which the scan table's shape — a flat array of regexes with no import of `src/` — does not allow today. Filed as a request. Open

**F632** — `contrast.ts` excludes `surface.bgDeep` from `textSurfaces` under a comment reading *`bgDeep` is excluded because it carries no text; if a surface ever paints text on it, that surface is wrong or the exclusion is* — and `plotToSvg` fills the whole page with `GROUND: ColourRef = "surface.bgDeep"` and then writes every axis label, node label, tile label and the reversed notice onto it. `tone.muted` on `bgDeep` is **3.02** dark and **2.44** light, the light one under `muted`'s own 2.5 floor, and C10's validation reports neither because the pairing it checks is `bg`/`bgElev`. The exclusion outlived the arm that arrived under it; C12 I112 clears 4.5 on both variants so nothing is unreadable today. Owed to C10. Open

**F610** — `b.plot`'s I69 guard read `drawn !== "graph"` — correct while `graph` was the member's only reader, and kept when C04 I92 gave `sankey` the same member *at both gates*. The validator admitted `{ form: "sankey", graph }` and the builder threw; T2.38 asks one gate. A string comparison against the first consumer's name is the configuration this guard outlived. **Fixed**

**F576** — eight spinner sets were refused on `EA=Ambiguous` before roadmap 51 made ambiguous width a capability and gave `SPINNER_SETS` a `narrowOnly` tier; only `growVertical` had crossed. Every frame of all eight measured 1 narrow / 2 wide, and three rows had named a subset of frames as ambiguous where every frame is. Seven sets plus `pulse` added; T2.70–T2.75 green, mutation kills T2.70. **Fixed**. (`typescript-eslint`'s peer range, F575, is the same shape — a refusal that outlived the compiler it was written against — and is keyed in group 10 with its count)

**F521** — `waffleGrid` rounded each segment independently against `pos < 100` — the identity at `scale === 1`, which was the corpus's only waffle for a year. `50/50/1` lost its sliver and `1/1/1` drew a hundredth square the legend did not name, in both arms, from one shared function the table had correctly ruled *shared*. Largest-remainder with the earlier segment winning a tie, C12 I108; 22 baselines. **Fixed**

**F523** — `annotationMarks` opened `if (a.kind !== "line") return []` under a comment reading *this arm does not draw annotations*, false from the day `line` crossed; `band`, `confidence` and `whiskers` never reached the SVG, and the sweep blamed `fill` for a mark that was not there. C12 I109; six baselines. **Fixed**

**F525** — the violin's IQR box sits on `layer: "annotation"` for its width, not its meaning — the layer is what escapes `SLOT_SHARE` — and painting claims translucent behind the data made it vanish into its density. Told apart by `seriesIndex`, which no claim carries; nineteen baselines. **Fixed**, with the honest fix — a rect that declines the inset without borrowing a layer — owed

**F529** — `yScale: "symlog"` and `"time"` reached their tick algorithms for the first time and moved no sample — a scale that selects ticks without placing samples is a scale in name. Ruled by the follow-through as C04 I81: the range carries the scale and both arms place through it. **Fixed**

**F506** — F498 merged the two sample grids into one and removed `sampleGrid`'s rung parameter; **`80 × 48` went on being the recorded grid in five places afterwards** — the comment heading the function whose parameter went away, C12 §3al's table, I84, commitment 84, the `PR7`/`CAM4` rows, and every performance budget in the 3D design note. It returns `160 × 192`: **30,720 samples against the recorded 3,840, eight times**, and a 120 KB depth buffer recorded as 15. **The implementation and its test were both corrected and none of the prose was** — `PR7`'s body asserts the new rule and explains why, while the spec entry naming that row still says its control is *that the two arms disagree*, a control that cannot be constructed because there is one arm. **`PR7` and `CAM4` are the same row under two ids** and `CAM4` is in no test file, which is where the correction stopped: the duplicate is not merely redundant, it is the copy that stays wrong. This is F498's own ruling — *when the thing a derived constant compensates for goes away, the compensation is the defect* — pointed at the documents, by the finding that named the mechanism and did not sweep for it

**F504** — a catalogue spreads a rung's override last so a rung can *replace* a field rather than only add to one — correct for sixteen members and wrong for the seventeenth, because `camera` is the one override that is an **object whose fields are independent statements** (C04 I75 made it `Partial<Camera>` for that reason). A rung naming an elevation and a distance discards the azimuth the animation's phase lives in, so four of seventeen rungs draw stills under `/live` and thirteen orbit. **The type cannot say so** — `Partial<Camera>` spread over `Partial<Camera>` typechecks — and it **reads as deliberate on three of the four**, whose fixed azimuths carry comments recording the measurement that chose them. The fourth is what gives it away: it sets no azimuth at all and still does not turn

**F492** — a per-cell colour split, individually optimal in every cell, alternates on noise where a gradient puts two masks at nearly equal error, and the frame reads as vertical seams no cell contains. A raster is read as a **field** and no per-cell rule can see one; the remedy is a decision that cannot alternate rather than a better score

**F493** — a sample exactly on two triangles' shared edge has a zero weight in both, and floating point decides which gets `-1e-13` and drops it. Latent for the life of the fill, invisible at four sample rows a cell, and cross-hatching the surface at eight — **a numerical fault is found by the change that raises resolution, not by the one that introduces it**. The tolerance has to be proportional to the triangle's own area, since an absolute epsilon is wrong at both ends of a mesh's size range

**F494** — half-cell triangles `◢◣◤◥` chosen per cell by a score against the cell's own coverage **serrate**, because neighbouring cells disagree about the angle and a silhouette is read as a run. And the alphabet was wrong anyway: a cell is twice as tall as it is wide, so the hypotenuse is one row per column where the offending edges are three times shallower. Removed, with the reasoning kept, because the next reader will have the idea

**F495** — an aspect correction reading *a sample is square on the sub-cell arms*: true at 2×4, a lie at 2×8. The whole figure squashed, and **both the surface and the box squashed with it** because they are drawn from one basis — but they round differently, so the visible symptom was *the surface does not reach the braille bounds*. A disagreement between two things drawn from one basis cannot be a rasterisation artefact, which points at the basis in one step

**F496** — a classifier taking the nine-level ladder only where two sample columns agreed drew **three** eighths on a Gaussian and sent every other edge cell to a half-cell staircase. Making the ladder the default lifted it to 19 and **the picture barely changed**, which is the finding: a block element splits a cell with one horizontal line, so a run moves the boundary once per cell and a shallow silhouette is a staircase whatever the level count. **The ceiling is arithmetic, not tuning** — four iterations went into the quantisation before it was stated

**F497** — the reference frame's samples are kept out of the dot grid so the axis is not drawn twice, but its glyph is only drawn in a cell with **no** data dots, so the exclusion buys nothing and costs the data. A z axis in front of a dome took a black stripe from peak to base, every cell of it a correct `⢸` — cells lit, colours right, extent unchanged, and **no count-based assertion sees it**. Two wrong repairs first, the second **circular**: it judged the data's footprint using the frame's own samples

**F498** — a marker tier table written as *doubled in each axis* to compensate for two sampling grids, kept after the two became one. **When the thing a derived constant compensates for goes away, the compensation is the defect.** Beside it, points and surfaces sharing one `kind`, so a mark quantised to eighths changed size with where in the cell it fell; and a compose writing sample row `y` into dot row `y`, an identity while the two agreed and a doubling once they did not

**F499** — one dot grid asked *is this cell the data's?* and *what does it draw?*. Different questions: the first must exclude the frame's samples and the second must not

**F500** — one array carrying both a surface's density glyph and the frame's stroke, with the local that read it named `framed`. Giving the frame its own structure looked like taking out everything the array held, so the argument was **replaced** rather than joined and a surface on the ASCII rung drew **nothing** — blank rather than degraded. **A channel with two owners wants two names**, or the next person to split it splits it in favour of whichever one the identifier mentions

**F501** — the plot's ground became a *foreground* the day a top-mass cell had to be drawn as its complement with the colours exchanged. A reader taking foregrounds alone sees a figure lit from above that is nothing of the kind, and a **headlight**, whose whole claim is that it has no direction, measured 1.24. `parseLine` had carried `background` all along — the frame was right and the reading was half of it. With it, a fixture that had stopped having its property: 160 faces over 72 cells, so both shading arms saturate and the row asserts the size of the frame

**F502** — three rows whose premises had been true and were not, and **a ruling falsified by a font**. F204 removed a guessed dot radius and ruled that braille emit a `<text>` *exactly as box drawing does*; `fc-list ":charset=2800"` returns no mono face, so braille alone fell through to a proportional font and every frame since showed that fallback's design. **Right about the defect and wrong about the remedy** — what survives both is geometry that is not a guess

---


**F792** — `ToolCallSpec.output` stays unconsumed and the equality arm demanded a third exemption go — `UNCONSUMED_FUNCTIONS.toolCallDoc` expired at its first `src/` call

**F798** — *any input disarms `⌃c`* met *1003 reports every move* — a resting hand would have made the double-`⌃c` exit unreachable; `button: "none"` does not disarm (T3.8d)

<a id="singles"></a>

**Keyed here by the F142 inventory sweep** — the second cohort, filed after this document's own count was last derived. Placed by mechanism from the entry; SP6 proves coverage and not placement, which is its stated limit.

| | why this group |
|---|---|
| **F378** | **the dither, for any figure with a painted ground** | `plotToSvg` → `b.image` at `imageProtocol: "none"` gives **511 of 784 cells inked** and no findable curve. The SVG paints its own `#141414` — right for a browser — so the PNG's mean luminance is **22 of 255**, and an ordered dither turns a flat 8% ground into a regular 8%-dense texture the figure is then drawn inside. **A pairing rather than a defect in either part**: C09 I36's dither is built for photographs, which have no flat ground, and a rendered figure is nearly all ground. Consumer-side remedy measured — a `linear` map of 20 → 0 takes it to **42 inked cells** and the curve appears. Recorded in `examples/plots`, because normalising a figure's ground is the caller's knowledge about their own picture |
| **F375** | **a bar's value labels, at a dashboard's density** | `4.17.4` is `4.1` and `7.4`; `3.83.4` is two numbers; `4.34.16.8` is three. A reader does not see a crowded row, they see **one number that is not any of the values**. **The same renderer has the rule one label row away** — `columnLabels` guards the category names with `start >= cells(row)` and drops rather than abut — so this is a missing clause, not an open question, with the drop-or-abut choice already made beside it. Found by looking at the demo at the width its own layout gives a bar: 53 cells, four categories, four series |


## Singles — one consumer each

| | verdict |
|---|---|
| **F731** | lanes7's last two owed items in numbers — the overprint was **six frames and ten pairs**, not the two reported, every pair **2.415 px** apart and **every one inside its own `viewBox`**, so the containment row agreed with all ten: *containment is not correctness*, measured. C12 I48's first two clauses carry to the image arm **unchanged**, because their reason is what a reader needs rather than what a cell is; its third — two on one row resolving to the later with a one-cell mark — **does not**, because both alternatives it rejected were rejected against a row count a fixed canvas does not have. **Two callouts contending was measured rather than built**: zero instances, four frames carry two callouts and the closest pair is **84.757 px**. The row is **carried from the ink** rather than recomputed, a second pure function being one product written twice — the mechanism already recorded for a reserve disagreeing with its own fit at equality. **Six of 244 frames moved, ten text elements removed, nothing added and no coordinate changed.** Cites every entry F725–F730 by number |
| **F716** | the remedy was scoped to a new helper in `src/presentation/theme/**`, and **the import edge was checked by nobody**: `Span`, `slot` and `wash` all live in `src/presentation/blocks/paint.ts`, and `paint.ts:16` imports `../theme/index.js`. So a `Span`-returning helper in `theme/` is `theme → blocks/paint → theme`, the cycle MG1 and MG22 exist to refuse, and the helper's natural home beside `wash` is a file the brief did not grant. **Resolved by moving the helper's subject rather than its home**: `isPictureGlyph` is a pure code-point predicate, so it lives in `theme/` with no import of `blocks/` at all |
| **F717** | a type that refuses a text-bearing picture cell was the shape asked for. `SankeyCell` is `{ text: string; ref?: ColourRef; background?: ColourRef }` and it inhabits **both** the picture cells and the label cells — `sankeyArea` pushes `{ text: ch }` for every character of a node's label — so **half its inhabitants are text and the type cannot forbid text**. The expressible guarantee is a discriminated union, and `definition.ts` reads `.text`, `.ref` and `.background` off it, so that union cannot land without a file this lane does not own. **What holds today is structural and undocumented**: `cellOf` is reached only where `text[r]?.[c]` is `undefined`, so a label character can never acquire a background — one `if` in one function, and nothing states it. Left owed with both blocker files named |
| **F722** | three lanes in **one checkout** rather than three worktrees, so a lane's report of a red gate is a report about the other lanes' edits. Named rather than absorbed: 8 × SP5 on `C12_plot_renderer.md` from finding numbers that arrived this session and were withdrawn before the merge, MA4 naming `c12-arm-seam.mjs`, and `SB3`'s ten moved SVG frames — every one an SVG-arm form in a file this lane did not touch. **A red row a lane did not cause belongs in its report as such**, and the alternative — each lane silently discounting what it cannot attribute — is how a real failure gets three independent excuses |
| **F724** | lanes6's three owed items in numbers — `isWide`, **8,619** code points Wide in the property and measuring one cell in the default mode across 65 runs, plus **369** narrowed and measured safe (none with `Emoji_Presentation=Yes`), and a second overlap where the geometry deviation claimed **49** the property calls Wide, so its own figure drops **625 to 576**; after the sweep zero under-counts in both modes and every remaining disagreement one of three recorded deviations, **602 = 576 + 26**; zero goldens moved and that was measured twice. The SVG callout — **13 strings in 7 frames** off the page, worst **240.5 px or 38%** of the figure in the fixture whose own comment named that case, rendering as two characters of thirty-six; the 0.6 advance ratio below every measured face (0.6001 to 0.6182) so it became a **bound at 0.65** with the falsification stated; **10 of 244** frames moved, not every one. The picture cell — the request's premise false three ways, both sites always carrying a glyph and the glyph load-bearing, so the admission moved **from the text to the alphabet** and the new reason survives a non-blank glyph where *carries no text* never could; **all twelve frame hashes identical**. Cites every entry F693–F723 by number |
| **F678** | `fill="${ground ?? ink}"` at `svg.ts:1152` and `:1320` reads as an ordinary defensive fallback, and `ink` at those two sites is **the fill of the rect the label sits on**. So if `surface.bg` ever failed to resolve, the label would be painted in exactly the colour behind it — **a ratio of 1.00 by construction, the one state §4g exists to forbid**. Unreachable today (`surface.bg` is required by the type and `validateTokens` refuses a non-hex surface), so recorded rather than repaired: a fallback whose failure mode is its own rule's negation |
| **F683** | replacing a hand-written subset with the full property was expected to only widen the answer toward the truth. It classifies **U+E0100..U+E01EF (VARIATION SELECTOR-17..256) as Ambiguous** — `Mn` combining marks that `isZeroWidth` did not list, so the derived table would have measured a combining mark at **two cells** at `wide`, an over-count the hand-written table could not have had because it never reached plane 14. Found in the by-hand classification table, in the cell where *zero-width* and *ambiguous* both claim a code point, before the sweep was re-run. **Ask what the new data reaches that the old data could not** is a different question from *is the new data right*, and only the first one finds this |
| **F666** | the three owed items in numbers — the page surface, 27 slots by three themes against both grounds, dark and high-contrast clearing either and light failing twelve times with `muted` at 2.44 under its own 2.5 floor, the move to `surface.bg` costing dark 5.5 per cent uniformly, gaining light 17, changing no token and no floor and moving 212 of 244 SVG frames in four roles at 982 occurrences; SP10, 183 mnemonic rows in 23 families with 358 commits swept and no duplicate beyond the one that motivated it, protecting 666 citations, its mutation pass finding that unwiring any SP rule failed nothing; markdown, three tiers not six, a quote rail rather than a glyph slot, U+23B8 measured at one cell under both conventions, and the nesting cap kept on the opposite reason to the recorded one. Cites every entry F649–F665 by number |
| **F667** | two records of one entry's status — `PART` in the Order column against `BUILT` in the evidence table, pushed and passing, because the resolver reads the evidence row only for its citations. The rule found a second instance, entry 7, older than this session. Blind spot stated: two records that agree and are both wrong are nobody's |
| **F655** | T2.27 (the page is a text surface) and T2.28 (four sites, one colour) might have been one claim twice, and the **mutation** is what says they are not: reverting `GROUND` to `surface.bgDeep` fails T2.27 on the first frame — `expected [ '#1a1a1a', '#222222' ] to include '#141414'` — and leaves T2.28 **green**, because all four sites move together. A mutation killing both would have meant one row was a restatement. Recorded because the check is cheap and the conclusion is not available from reading the two rows |
| **F658** | the counter-example to the usual direction — a gate that **is** run and did catch something, on a file nothing else in the suite reads. `MA4`'s equality arm resolves every `expect` string in every mutation run file against a test path the run executes, and it failed on two compound expectations naming both the SP10 row and the SP7 row the same mutation kills (`"SP10: … / SP7: …"`), which no single test name contains. The information is real — one mutation failing on both sides is the evidence the implementation is shared rather than copied — and it belongs in the comment rather than in the field a resolver reads |
| **F662** | the **ordered**-list arm is where the depth collapse was invisible in principle, which is why the over-cap mark applies to both arms: a bullet list's depth 3 and depth 4 differ in their text, while an ordered list's differ in nothing a reader could attribute to depth at all — the number says *which* item and never *how deep*. So the ordered arm gains a gutter only at the depth where it has something to say |
| **F648** | the three residues in numbers — F591 fixed, 102 of 560 pairs to 0 with three golden frames moved and the terminal baseline none, the repair subsuming F590's arm and surfacing a lost content character; `unitsOf` de-duplicated, 122 hunk-by-layout rows and 481 units byte-identical, an over-stated unit invisible to 99 rows; 33 sankey labels at worst 1.02 against floors of 2.5 and 4.5, ruled `tone.default` on a `bgDeep` halo at 7.59–12.43 where the halo alone left `muted` at 2.44; cites every entry F630–F647 by number |
| **F630** | every sankey SVG label was under every floor — 33 labels, six variants, both themes, worst **1.02** and best 1.41 against `muted`'s own 2.5 and body text's 4.5 — where the brief expected some fine and some not. Placement could not have helped: the label is already in d3's place and `x0 = xOf(la) + nodeW` puts the ribbon at the bar's edge, so *outside the bar* and *on the ribbon* are one region and no node in the corpus has bare ground beside it. C12 I112, §3ap.4 K15 and §3ap.7. **Fixed** |
| **F636** | the enforcer **does** catch a bare invariant in a mutation run file — `make enforce` went red only once `c12-sankey.mjs` carried a bare `I112`, suggesting `C09` because the run file is in neither `OWNERS` nor `TOPICS` and the resolver falls back. Recorded for the contrast with the duplicate-row-name gap: the rule that resolves a *citation* is exact and the rule that would resolve a *definition* does not exist |
| **F641** | cross-lane observation, recorded so the lead does not read it as lane U's: `make enforce` reported 2 violations on one run and 1 on the next during the lane, both SP9 naming `C12 I112` — an invariant landed by another lane between the two runs. No C25 violation at any point |
| **F642** | F590's arm swallowed a space strictly **inside** an atom: `wrapCellsParts("aaa bbb ccc", 7, "narrow", [{ from: 0, to: 11 }])` gave `aaa bbb` / `ccc`, dropping the space at offset 7 from inside the valued run. `breakPoint` returns `null` there — both interior spaces are inside the atom — so the no-break-point arm fired. Every existing row passed: each row is still an exact slice from its `start`, so C04 I86 holds, and the source is one character shorter with nothing measuring that. Found by the walk table's atom row having no ruling, not by a test; `aaa bbb` / `" ccc"` after. **Fixed** |
| **F629** | the seven owed items in numbers — `measure` 0.6 ms for 50 000 table rows and paint ~0.25 ms/row so 2 000 is a policy; the atom wrap's leading-space defect fixed and its one-word-early sibling recorded; LCS capped at 200 a side and an unrelated pair drawing nothing; sankey 14/11, 60 frames, 6 SVGs, `▒` chosen on the 1-bit frame; GIF 8 frames / 0 pixels against `sharp`, 116 509 B once against 29 662 B a tick; cites every entry F588–F628 by number |
| **F590** | a full row followed by a space began the next row with the space — `wrapCells("abcdef gh", 6)` → `["abcdef", " gh"]` — reachable in prose only when a token exactly filled the row and in the ordinary case once a valued atom does. One arm in `wrapCellsParts`: a space overflowing a row with no break point *is* the break. 2 380 committed frames compared, 0 moved. **Fixed** |
| **F591** | the wrapper breaks one word early when a row is exactly full — `"aa bb cc dd"` at 5 gives `aa` / `bb` / `cc dd` where `aa bb` / `cc dd` fits — the overflow check firing on the space and the break search taking the last space *inside* the full row. **Fixed 2026-09-04**, and it *subsumed* F590's arm rather than sitting beside it — F590 had written the same rule twice and ignored the search's answer. The deferral's reason was an estimate: it moves **three** golden frames, not every paragraph, and the terminal baseline moves none; a 560-pair sweep went 102 violations to 0 |
| **F592** | `RunContext.capabilities` could be `Pick<TerminalCapabilities, "colourDepth">` — the painter reads the depth only — and takes the full record because `paint.tone()` does and every caller holds one. Noted so the narrowing is known to be free the day a caller has only a depth |
| **F593** | another lane's files were failing `tsc` while lane T ran — `image-frames.test.ts:440`, `block-cap.test.ts`'s `Windowed` — so the lane's typecheck verdict was filtered, not clean. Recorded so the report's green is read as *this lane's files* |
| **F595** | three groupings of one pairing: `definition.ts`'s `runsOf`, `window.ts`'s `unitsOf` (with `rowSlice` pairing the *n*th remove with the *n*th add implicitly), and the lane's writer. The first is now C04's `changedRuns` and the writer reads it, so diff and drawing agree by construction; `unitsOf` still walks its own boundaries and agrees today — SS30's shape. Owed with the symbol: `unitsOf` → `changedRuns` |
| **F596** | a writer emitting a run's diffed removes then its diffed adds reorders `-a +b -c +d` into `-a -c +b +d` — a different document in unified layout and a different `Hunk` for the gate. The first draft of `intralineLines` did exactly this; caught re-reading `changedRuns`'s contract before `tsc` ran, from the walk's R3×R6 cell. The landed writer maps over `lines` with a replacement `Map` and cannot reorder; T1.14 pins it. **Fixed** before compiling |
| **F597** | the unrelated-pair ruling has a precedent and the precedent's number is taste: delta's `max-line-distance` defaults to 0.6, git's `--color-words` has none. Ruled at **zero shared non-whitespace tokens**, the one point where the underline is provably vacuous — every cell of both lines would carry it; anything above is a preference no measurement here settles. C25 I10 names the precedent so a later reader moves it deliberately |
| **F599** | `textSpans` slices tokens by the stripped length over tokens of the raw text — `truncateParts` computes `kept` over `stripControl(text)` while `tokenise` runs over `text`, so each C0/C1 byte before the cut shifts the token slice one code unit short. Pre-existing, rare in a diff line, and the span path is immune by construction. Belongs with C09's `sliceTokens` contract; noted, not fixed |
| **F600** | a lane's own gate reads red for reasons that are not its own — 39 enforce violations of which 2 were lane P's, 37 other lanes'; and between two runs with no edit from this lane the count grew by 20. The only way to attribute is to grep the report for the lane's paths. Recorded as the figure for the shared-tree method |
| **F602** | paint is linear with no knee — first paint of `window [0, N)` at N = 500 / 1 000 / 2 000 / 5 000 is ~0.25 ms a row for every kind within ±30%, so halving the cap halves the cost at every size and no figure picks the default. 2 000 is a **policy** — fifty screens of forty — pinned to `MAX_ROWS`'s existing figure so the tree has one number; recorded as a policy in C14 §4b and `DEFAULT_MAX_BLOCK_ROWS`'s comment so nobody reads the table as having chosen it |
| **F603** | a capped 50 000-line `code` block still tokenises the whole text on entry — `window [0, 500)` is 1 696 ms first paint, 196 steady — because the window keeps `text` whole for the `lineRange` pin. At `from === 0` the pin is unnecessary; the cut belongs in `kinds/code.ts` (C09), owed there and recorded in C14 §4b and C09 §2b |
| **F605** | D40's marker costs a block, so a store with `cap: 2` holding three one-block entries evicts **two** — `sweep` loops `while (live + marker() > cap)` and the marker counts once anything has dropped. C13 §5 says so and the fixture had not read it; corrected to `cap: 4`. No defect, recorded because the first reading would have been baked into a test |
| **F606** | `tools/mutate/anchors.mjs` checks `corpus.includes(expect)` for the whole string, and no run puts two rows in one `expect` — so `"T1.20, T6.22"` reads as unreachable. Eight of eleven expectations in `c14-cap.mjs` were rewritten to one row each. A convention nothing states |
| **F607** | the registry's `#form` for a capped block measures the whole block, windows it, measures the window, and `render` calls `#form` twice more — two extra whole measures and two windows per render for a capped block, bounded by F601's ≤ 21 ms at 50 000 rows and cached per entry by C22 I58. Accepted rather than missed; recorded so a profile that finds it knows |
| **F608** | a background `npm run build` started in the same turn as a hand mutation compiled the mutation into `dist/`; nothing wrong shipped because `dist/` was rebuilt after the restore, before any probe read it. The converse of *never edit `src` while a mutation pass runs*: never start anything that reads `src` while a hand mutation is live |
| **F611** | adding `sankey` to the form union fails nine count assertions in eight files — `plot-detail-scope` 45, `plot-hierarchy-gate` 43, `plot-mutations` 32 and 40, `plot-tree` 46, `plot-figure` 122, `plot-arm-unification` 47 and 238, `plot-arm-disagreement`'s eight — each a *fixture responds* counter restating the record's size. The deliberate cost of *count an exemption, do not exclude it*; each moved with a comment naming `sankey` so the next form's lane finds the trail |
| **F612** | the only terminal sankey found — `mermaid-ascii`'s `pkg/sankey/renderer.go` — is a flow table, one line per flow with the bar's length the value and no node placement. Not a layout to compare against, but it settled the numerals: **no** value beside the bar, because the ribbon's width is the reading and a numeral per bar is the table that renderer draws instead of a figure. §3ap.1 records the source |
| **F613** | two ribbon interiors drawn — `█` + `dim` and `▒` — and the 1-bit frame chose: with colour stripped, A is one block of `█` with letters in it, the bar column indistinguishable from the fill because `dim` is an attribute; B is two shapes at every depth, and at 24-bit the medium shade reads as d3's half-opacity ribbon. I17 decided it; SK7 asserts the 1-bit unicode frame **equals** the 24-bit frame with its SGR removed |
| **F614** | `crowded` at gap 0 stacks two half-row bars per row, both centring on the same row, so the second label collides with the first and is dropped — `src-05 · 07 · 09 · 11` named and their neighbours drawn unnamed. Correct under K3's rule applied vertically, found by reading the frame and not by the table, which had K3 (labels × width) and K5 (bars × height) as separate rows; now K8 |
| **F615** | `▀ ▄ █ ▒` are all `East_Asian_Width=Ambiguous`, so there is no width-stable half-block and `sankeyAlphabet` has two arms, not three. SK7 asserts the wide frame **equals** the ASCII frame rather than *contains no wide glyph* — an equality, because an absence assertion is satisfied by an empty frame |
| **F617** | reference tooling on the host: `matplotlib` fails on the NumPy 2 ABI, plotly imports with no `kaleido`, and the container has no plotting stack. The reference picture was d3-sankey's committed `energy.png`; `tools/refdiff/reference.py` gains a `SKIPPED` row for `sankey` (matplotlib's `Sankey` is a single-node flow diagram) and RD5's recorded set gains the form |
| **F618** | other lanes' state seen from lane G — `tsc` red on `session.ts:39` / `image-frames.test.ts:30` (`framesOf` not exported) and `spans.test.ts:47` (arity); MA4 38 anchors missing across four runs, none in `c12-graph.mjs`; SP1/SP3 rows on `C22 I77` lane V's. SS47 on `sankey.ts` closed with a `MARK_EXEMPTIONS` row; SS54 R20 expired as designed |
| **F626** | files touched outside lane V's ownership list, each with why: `construct.ts` (the `Frames` store's binding, which no lane owned), `blocks/types.ts` and `render-lines.ts` (`RenderContext.frames`), `transmit-image.ts` (a GIF under `f=100` draws nothing), `b.image`'s signature check in `builders/index.ts`; `image-halfblock.test.ts` **not** edited, the refusal's wording chosen to keep HB8's substring |
| **F627** | `git checkout <path>` on a tree five lanes share restores the index, not *my change*: had another lane's edit to `validate.ts` landed between the lane's patch and its checkout it would have been wiped without a trace. Four checkouts, none known to have destroyed anything, and that is luck. The right form is a reverse edit with an asserted anchor — which the untracked `gif.ts` and `frames.ts` forced anyway, and one mutation in `frames.ts` was live for one tool call after a checkout "restore" refused |
| **F587** | the owed three in numbers — the `code`/`raw` window 934 → 34 ms and 7 624 → 38 ms, five refusals re-ruled once each, spans over 259 files with no golden moved; cites every entry F567–F586 by number |
| **F567** | C09 §6b refused `code` a window on a correct measurement and reasoned about the wrong remedy — a wider slice or a highlighter's state on a public type — with the third option missing: *do not slice*. The window is the same `text` reference and two integers; nothing about the tokeniser, the memo or the slot map changed. **Fixed** |
| **F570** | two rows called T3.19 in C14 — an older edge row the spec never listed and Lane F's I23 row took the same number; implemented beside it as `T3.19 (I23)`. The older one wants a number the spec knows about |
| **F571** | the before-figures differ by machine and both are real — 1 406 ms there, 934 ms here for the same 2 000 `code` lines, `logs` 21 against 14 — so C14 §4a carries both and reads every before/after pair on one machine |
| **F572** | SP2 caught I82 inserted beside its subject (I67/I68) while Lane S had numbered from I83, so the file read `… I67 I82 I68 … I81 I83 …`. Moved to document order; not a defect in either lane |
| **F579** | the audit's closing list verified at HEAD and not touched — mermaid theming registered as owed (R23/R24), the four 3D retraction sites and the SVG horizon confirmed corrected, B17 and the popup `none` arm still true refusals |
| **F580** | Ink re-encodes SGR — one escape per attribute, closed in reverse, colour as its own pair — so the frame rows assert Ink's form and the `1;3;4` order claim is asserted at `paint()`, where it is made |
| **F582** | `runsOf`'s first snap went upward and collapsed any span starting inside a leading cluster; T3.64 (C04 I84) caught it before it shipped and outward — `from` to the cluster's start, `to` to its end — landed. **Fixed** before shipping |
| **F583** | the markdown `DELIMITER` regex accepts one column, so `\|---\|` reads as a table. Entry 11's residue, not the spans arc's; recorded, not changed |
| **F566** | the residue pass in numbers — two of three motivating claims disproven, 72 record corrections, ~250 frames, five enforcement rules, and the substrate items; cites every entry above by number |
| **F565** | one block's paint is linear in the block — 2 000 lines of `code` 1 406 ms, 20 000 lines 11 360 ms, `logs` 30 ms — and the roadmap's 83 ms was a different method, so both are recorded side by side in C14 §4a. I23 is written as a target false for `code` and `raw` today; T2.22 pins the set so the window cannot land silently |
| **F524** | three defects in the first draft of the annotation crossing, each found by reading a frame — the dash was the layer's and had to be the mark's, a closed dashed interior outlined the ceiling, and *behind* means *first* in an arm that paints in order. **Fixed** |
| **F526** | `radarAsciiRows` wrote its residue over the last shown category and under-counted by one; `segmentLegend`, one function up, reserves the row first. **Fixed** |
| **F527** | the pie legend's residue was truncated by the entries it stood in for — `⋯ 5 m…` — and the disagreement reader took the `m` for a segment's name. **Fixed** |
| **F530** | five fixture notes from the frames — a heatmap key showing the whole ramp over a zero span, `near-clip` a wall by construction, `bar/constant` unable to reach the zero-span arm, the radar's circle grid needing the line style, the constant surface colouring through its own ramp. Recorded in the fixtures |
| **F549** | two test ids collided with rows already in C22, twice, caught by SP7 each time; *next letter after the last one I read* is wrong in a spec another lane is appending to |
| **F560** | `mosaic.test.ts` names nine tests `MG1`…`MG9`, homonyms of the module-graph family; `MS1`…`MS9` requested |
| **F511** | **The GPU ceiling is under half the frame, and the raster is not what costs.** The orbit frame after the cache: bunny raster 55.9 ms = 46%, suzanne raster 1.9 ms = 15%, and the rest is string work the GPU cannot touch. A GPU that did the raster in zero saves 46% of the bunny and 15% of suzanne; a warm `worker_threads` pool round-trips in 0.049 ms but the rasteriser is a project, clip, barycentric, shade pipeline that no flat buffer can carry. **The cheaper path is decimation** — 3.1× fewer faces for 2.2× speed and 6.4% of inked cells moved — and it is a picture decision, not an engineering one |
| **F508** | `drawnOf` collects a block's triangles with `tris.push(...trianglesOf(…))`, and **a spread is an argument list**: 100,000 elements is fine, 125,000 throws `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded`. C12 I11's shape is that the renderer answers rather than refuses — its own §3230 row reads *I11 says the renderer never throws* — and this throws from an expression that reads as a concatenation. Never fired because the largest mesh in the tree is 69,451 faces, under half the ceiling; `parseObj` fans quads, so a 63k-quad model is not. **A stack overflow rather than a bounds error**, so nothing between it and the frame can name a mesh size. A loop is the whole fix |
| **F8** | omitting `env` stops the shell opening rather than degrading it — **real, small**: the spec says it degrades and it does not |
| **F12** | `npm publish --registry` is accepted and ignored — a fact about npm, recorded so nobody re-derives it |
| **F19** | `ManifestDocument` accepts the one value construction refuses — **fixed** |
| **F31** | `yFormat: "percent"` expects a fraction — **real**, and the reason `b.plot` exposes the pin and not the format |
| **F53** | `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` makes an optional field unsupplyable — **real**, and it rhymes with F58b: both are the type surface fighting the consumer rather than a missing feature |
| **F177** | a shared precision **the string threw away** — `yLabels` computed one precision for the axis and `Number(v.toFixed(2))` stripped the trailing zero, so `0.20 0.15 0.10` rendered as `0.2 0.15 0.1`. The survey's own counter-example, shipped. **The prose was true about the half that worked**, which is why reading it agreed. **Fixed** — a named precision is kept, an unnamed one trimmed |
| **F178** | a nice step of **zero**, and a render that never returns — a denormal span underflows, `floor(min / 0) * 0` is `NaN`, and `drawLine` stops on `x === ex` where `NaN` equals nothing. **The invariant is two modules from the decision** (C12 I2), which no rule-interaction table for the function reaches. Two further lessons: a plausible constant (`return 1`) terminated and swamped the data by 300 orders, and a float loop counter at 10³⁰⁰ does not advance. **Fixed** |
| **F180** | **the heatmap was unreachable from the builder** — `b.plot` hardcoded `form: "line"` and `b.spark` writes `"sparkline"`, so `"heatmap"` was buildable by nothing public. That is the whole of *the heatmap has data and no drawing*: a consumer could not have been written. **MG27 passed it** because the rule asks whether the constructed literal *mentions* the field, and a closed union with one hardcoded arm satisfies a check about names — F84's shape one rule along. Second instance in the same edit: `plot.xLabels`'s exemption had two clauses and one expired. **Fixed** |
| **F179** | the accessibility theme had **no `categorical` palette**, so every multi-series plot drew eight series in one colour — silent, because a missing family and a decoration family collapsed at one bit are the same `NO_STYLE`. Found by F172's resolve-time gate on its first run. **The rule that can fire is worth more than the case it was written for**, and that case has not happened yet. **Fixed** |
| **F182** | **F175's own fix, applied to the two arms it was found on and not to the class.** `formatReadout` gave `percent` and `fraction` a precision and left the default arm on `decimalsFor`, which answers *how many digits does an axis label at this scale want* — so a `bar` with no `yFormat` drew `45` for `45.2` while the identical block with `yFormat: "percent"` drew `45.2%`. Same function, same values, one dropping the digit it exists to keep. `categorical.ts`'s comment — *this was the fourth* — is accurate about the **call site** and closes off the question of whether the function is right on every arm. **260 golden frames pass unchanged across the fix**, so no committed frame exercises a plot number with a fraction and no `yFormat`. Underneath it, a second on a *set*: four readings of one quantity at four precisions, F177 where nothing had named a shared precision because nothing had formatted a set. **Fixed** — the numeric arm keeps what the producer sent, and `readoutSet` names the set |
| **F175** | a bar's number was formatted as a **tick label** — `Math.round`, right for an axis mark and wrong for a readout, so `45.2%` drew as `45%`. Rhymes with F31 one field over: the `yFormat` enum is shared on purpose and *precision is not a property of the unit*. **Fixed** with `formatReadout`, a named intent rather than an optional argument — the argument version was already passed by `yLabels` and widened every percent axis in the corpus |
| **F176** | the `fill` pair had **no ambiguous-width arm**, so `█ ░ —` drew at twice their cells and `truncate` ate the bar's number. Exactly what `RAMP_UNICODE` did and what `ladderFor` swaps braille in for; the fourth encoding axis was added last and did not inherit the third's fix. **The golden corpus had recorded it** — `table-value-bar` at `dark-wide` read `█░░░░░░░ …` in a committed snapshot nobody looked at. **Fixed** |
| **F72** | `ls -p` does not mark a symlinked directory, so the one thing a user wants to do with a directory is the thing it prevents — **fixed** with `ls -1pL`, measured out of a real container's listing |
| **F484** | `markers3()` holds five shapes at three depth tiers, and at the **far** tier there is nothing to pick between — `· ∙ • ˙ ‧` is one dot drawn five slightly different ways. So `Point3Series.marker` is honoured at two tiers of three and ignored at the third, which is correct (at that size a shape is not a channel) and invisible: a caller sets `marker: "square"`, sees squares near and dots far, and reports a bug against behaviour that is right. **A limit of the alphabet and not of the dispatch**, which is what decides where it is written down — with the table, and in C12 I99, rather than in a `// TODO` beside the lookup |

**Keyed here by the F142 inventory sweep** — the second cohort, filed after this document's own count was last derived. Placed by mechanism from the entry; SP6 proves coverage and not placement, which is its stated limit.

| | why this group |
|---|---|
| **F90** | the frame is recomputed and rewritten whole, and the four fixes are one chain |
| **F91** | every `b.live` part owns its own fetch, so two views of one source disagree |
| **F98** | the suite leaves eleven sessions' process handlers attached |
| **F100** | narrowing the adapter's return exposed three things the wide type hid |
| **F103** | three renderers implement one pattern and none of them names it |
| **F122** | the framework holds apps to a rule it exempts itself from |
| **F137** | a fold runs on a version, so an attempt that failed is not counted |
| **F140** | a refusal that leaves a constructed graph |


## What this triage cannot see

**Most of what found things this step is not reachable by sorting a list.** Measured over
docker-tui's step 8, eleven findings, and **not one came from a test written to look for it**:
reading a frame (4), writing a second consumer from the public surface (3), the mutation pass
(2), an untouched file appearing in a diff (1), asking where a settled claim was written (1).

Every instrument that found something is **a way of looking rather than a thing asserted**, and
a triage is a thing asserted. Specifically:

- **Reading a green gate's counters instead of its exit status.** **F82 was found in the act
  of verifying this file**, and it is the newest instrument here: `make enforce` printed
  success through three separate vacuities in one rule, and each time the evidence was a
  number the rule already returned and nobody read. A triage sorts what a gate let through;
  it has no way to ask whether the gate looked.
- **A frame-read.** F62, F67, F74, F75, F79 — five of the last twenty-six. Nothing in a sorted
  list tells you a frame was never read, and F67 is the proof: golden frames sweep 60/80/120/160
  columns and the height axis has no equivalent, so a document rendering to zero visible rows
  produces a frame that is *correct*.
- **Writing a second consumer.** F7, F56, F58, F70. The ranking **is** consumer count, and
  ranking by it cannot produce one. `examples/minimal` is forty lines written from the public
  surface with no house style to copy, and it hit F58's wall on its first compile — which is the
  evidence that a thing is the API's shape rather than a habit.
- **A mutation pass.** It indicts tests, and sometimes prose: a sentence that cannot be violated
  reads exactly like one that is obeyed. A triage reads prose as given, which is A03 §2's
  vacuity class pointed at this document.
- **An untouched file appearing in a diff.** F56's mode change is evidence about a mechanism
  nobody knew was running. No category here would hold it.
- **Going to find where a claim was written** — group 10, and this is the self-referential one.
  **A triage is a fourth document that cites the other three.** The instrument with the highest
  yield in this repository is the one this file is most exposed to, because grouping and ranking
  is exactly the operation that lends a claim authority it has not earned.
- **A finding that was never filed at all.** Not hypothetical. **F81 spent a whole step as a
  comment** at `examples/docker/src/progress.ts:31`, ending *"filed rather than worked around"* — and it was not
  filed. So the strongest group on this list looked like three consumers rather than four, and
  nothing that sorts `FINDINGS.md` could have known. SP5 checks that every citation resolves to
  a finding that exists; **nothing checks whether something that should be a finding is one.**

---

## How this file was checked

**The inventory is derived, and now it is derived by something that runs.** `SP6` in
`tools/enforce/findings.mjs` takes the distinct ids from `FINDINGS.md`, takes the ids keyed in
the group sections here, and **compares the two sets by equality** — so a finding filed and not
keyed fails `make enforce`, and the sum in the ranking table is checked against what the groups
actually hold rather than against itself.

**It was a snapshot until then, and it had gone 55 findings stale.** This paragraph used to
read *"yields 89 ids and every one is keyed in a group above — 10 + 12 + … = 89"*. Measured
when SP6 was written: **145 distinct findings, 55 keyed nowhere**, and the sum still reached
89, so the arithmetic offered as evidence passed exactly as it did on the day it was true. That
is F87's proxy one level out — F87 found a total cannot see a duplicate placed twice and
counted once, and this is the same total unable to see an id never placed at all. F142.

**What the column counts, stated because it is not what the header used to say.** `ids keyed`
is *ids appearing in bold inside a group's section*. This document keys in two forms — a table
row's first cell, and bold in prose — and nothing distinguishes a key from a mention: table
cells alone find 78 and leave groups 6 and 13 keying nothing at all. So the number, and SP6,
prove **coverage and not placement**, exactly as SP5 checks a citation's existence and not its
aim. Tightening the key form to table rows only is real work and is not done here; until it is,
a finding bolded in passing inside a group it does not belong to is counted there.

**The groups are not disjoint, and an earlier version of this paragraph claimed they were.**
F30 is keyed in group 4 and again in group 7 — it genuinely is both a verdict the union lacks
and a drawing that asserted one — so group 7 names fifteen ids and counts fourteen, excluding
the one already counted. **The sum reaches 89 either way**, which is the point: a total over
group sizes cannot see a duplicate that was placed twice and counted once. That is F87, and it
is this document's own check failing the rule it was built from — *assert the artefact, not a
proxy*. A disjointness claim wants `sort | uniq -d`, which is one line and was never run.

That check exists because the F55 version had a heading reading *5 surfaces* over a row listing
six. **A count that disagrees with its own row reads as authority and is the cheapest possible
error to make** — which is also F65's finding, one document out. Deriving the list rather than
transcribing it is the pattern for any future inventory here; deriving it does not make it a
partition.

**The residue pass's 55 entries (F512–F566) were keyed in one move each**, on F434's rule: the row goes in the group and the same group's column moves by one, so the column sums to 570 because each of eight groups gained exactly what it keyed — 13, 10, 3, 6, 4, 7, 4 and 8 — and not because errors cancelled. Measured before the merge at 515 = 515 and after at 570 = 570, from SP6's own counters.

**The owed three's 21 entries (F567–F587) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 2 gained one (F586), 7 one (F578), 9 two (F568, F584), 10 four (F573, F574, F575, F577), 11 three (F569, F581, F585), 14 one (F576) and Singles nine — 1 + 1 + 2 + 4 + 3 + 1 + 9 = 21. F575 has group 14's shape as well as group 10's and is **mentioned** in 14 without bold, so it is keyed once and the column moves once; F434's failure mode is exactly the row bolded in both. Measured before at 570 = 570 and after at 591 = 591, from SP6's own counters.

**The seven owed items' 42 entries (F588–F629) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 2 gained one (F624), 7 one (F621), 9 five (F598, F609, F616, F622, F623), 10 three (F601, F619, F620), 11 six (F588, F589, F594, F604, F625, F628), 14 one (F610) and Singles twenty-five — 1 + 1 + 5 + 3 + 6 + 1 + 25 = 42. F619 and F628 are one defect from two sides — the claim and the row that stood for it — and are keyed in 10 and 11 respectively, each mentioned in the other without bold so the column moves once per id. Measured before at 591 = 591 and after at 633 = 633, from SP6's own counters.

**F667 is one more, keyed in Singles** (Singles 75 → 76, the ranking sum 670 → 671): the resolver
holding two records of one status and comparing neither. It is a single because its consumer is the
one instrument, and it is filed after the lanes because the lead found it while confirming a lane's
own roadmap edit had landed — the Order column still said `PART` where the evidence table said
`BUILT`, and `roadmap-status` printed *every claim resolves* on that tree.

**The three lanes' 19 entries (F630–F648) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 7 gained two (F631, F643), 9 four (F633, F638, F639, F646), 10 two (F640, F645), 11 five (F634, F635, F637, F644, F647), 14 one (F632) and Singles five — 2 + 4 + 2 + 5 + 1 + 5 = 19. F637 and F647 are one question from two sides — the blind spot and the check that narrowed it — and are both group 11's, keyed once each with the other mentioned without bold. Lane W's file numbered its own findings F591 and F593–F597, which are ledger numbers already: its F591 is the ledger's F591 (fixed and keyed since the seven owed items) and appears nowhere new, and its F593–F597 are renumbered to F642–F646. F591's body cited *what turned up F593* and now cites F642 — a citation that resolved against a real but unrelated finding, which is the class SP5 states it cannot see. Measured before at 633 = 633 and after at 652 = 652, from SP6's own counters.

**The three owed items' 18 entries (F649–F666) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 7 gained two (F650, F660), 9 three (F654, F663, F665), 10 one (F657), 11 six (F649, F651, F653, F656, F659, F664), 14 two (F652, F661) and Singles four — 2 + 3 + 1 + 6 + 2 + 4 = 18. F649 and F652 are C10 §4's one conditional from two sides — the surface it names and the palette it exempts — and are keyed in 11 and 14 respectively, each mentioned in the other without bold so the column moves once per id; F656 and F657 are the same rule's wiring and its yield, keyed in 11 and 10. **The lane-local ids collided with nothing this time and that was measured rather than assumed**: `C\d`, `E\d` and `M\d` cannot reach a two-digit spec id, no lane body cross-references its own lane, and the three genuine ledger citations the bodies carry (F382, F632 in lane C, F635 in lane E) were compared by equality before and after the rewrite. Measured before at 652 = 652 and after at 670 = 670, from SP6's own counters.

**lanes5's 25 entries (F668–F692) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 7 gained four (F668, F674, F681, F690), 9 seven (F672, F673, F679, F682, F684, F686, F692), 10 three (F671, F677, F680), 11 nine (F669, F670, F675, F676, F685, F687, F688, F689, F691) and Singles two (F678, F683) — 4 + 7 + 3 + 9 + 2 = 25, and group 14 gained none. **Three pairs are one defect from two sides**, each keyed once with the other mentioned without bold so the column moves once per id: F680 carries the row-by-row measurement of F665's count and F690 the lesson about which authority it was taken against, keyed in 10 and 7; F687 is `partSeparator`'s narrow parameter and F691 the fix and the gate it moved, **both group 11's**, on F637/F647's precedent; F688 is YA4 vacuous and F692 the three further layers underneath it, keyed in 11 and 9. **The lane-local ids were the hazard this time and it was measured rather than assumed**: lane B numbers its findings `FA`..`FJ`, which are **letters and not ledger numbers**, so the rewrite pattern is anchored on both sides (`(?<![-\w])F([A-J])(?![\w-])`), scoped to lane B's own entries, and the count asserted — measured with `grep -on`, both lanes mention their local ids **only in their own headings**, so no body cross-reference survived to be rewritten and `rewrites == 0` is an assertion rather than a hope. The five genuine ledger citations the bodies carry (F652, F653, F219 in lane I; F665, F292 in lane B) were compared by equality before and after. **F665's body was corrected in place**, its own lead correction having been wrong the same way the original was — *five of the ten* is three of ten, `«` and `»` Neutral and `⚠` over-counted — citing F680 for the measurement and F690 for the class. Measured before at 671 = 671 and after at 696 = 696, from SP6's own counters.

**lanes6's 32 entries (F693–F724) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 7 gained six (F697, F698, F711, F714, F718, F719), 9 eight (F693, F695, F699, F700, F708, F712, F720, F723), 10 five (F694, F696, F702, F706, F715), 11 eight (F701, F703, F705, F707, F709, F710, F713, F721), 14 one (F704) and Singles four (F716, F717, F722, F724) — 6 + 8 + 5 + 8 + 1 + 4 = 32. **Four pairs are one defect from two sides**, each keyed once with the other mentioned without bold so the column moves once per id: F700 is the gate firing on a code point and F723 the fix and why it is a named lookbehind rather than a wider class, **both group 9's**, on F687/F691's precedent; F693 is the Wide table wrong in both directions and F697 the deviation's own figure moving under it, keyed in 9 and 7; F703 is the thirteen clipped strings and F704 the constant that clipped them, keyed in 11 and 14; F714 is I21's condition being false of both its sites and F715 the mechanism the same sentences named and that does not exist at one of them, keyed in 7 and 10. **The lane-local ids collided with real ids this time and it was measured rather than assumed**: lane C numbers its findings `C1`..`C11` and its bodies cite the component `C12` twice, lane P numbers `P1`..`P9` and `P5` occurs twice inside `SP5`, so each pattern is anchored on both sides and **substitutes only when the captured id is in that lane's own map** — measured with `grep -on`, all three lanes mention their local ids **only in their own headings**, so `rewrites == 0` is an assertion rather than a hope, and the two `C12`s were compared by equality before and after. **Two lane sentences were reworded before merging, both asserted**: lane W's SP5 entry quoted the violation message verbatim, and a bare finding number in that form **is** the violation once it is in the ledger; and lane P's report of red rows said three numbers *which the ledger does not have*, which this merge assigns to lane W, so the sentence would have been false on the day it landed and the numbers would have resolved against real but unrelated findings. The `U+F900..U+FAFF` and `U+F000..U+FFFF` forms in F700's body were **kept deliberately**: they are the live control for F723's lookbehind, and SP5 must scan them without firing. Measured before at 696 = 696 and after at 728 = 728, from SP6's own counters.

**lanes7's 7 entries (F725–F731) were keyed the same way**, one move each: group 7 gained one (F726), 9 two (F728, F730), 10 one (F727), 11 one (F725), 14 one (F729) and Singles one (F731) — 1 + 2 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 7. **Two are one defect from two sides**, keyed once each with the other mentioned without bold: F725 is the overprint's size and F726 the third writer the same sweep found, keyed in 11 and 7 — the first is closed by the row that now exists and the second is forbidden a remedy by C12 I48, which is why they cannot share a key. **F729 is the judgement call and is keyed in 14 rather than 11**: its own stated point is that `RC1`'s shape cannot see label-against-label, which is group 11's, but the *mechanism* is a tick count configured in cells running against a fixed pixel canvas, which is this group's subject and is the half that will still be true after the row is written; it is mentioned in 11 without bold, so the column moves once. **The lane-local ids were F-numbers this time, which is the collision class at its sharpest**: lane O proposed F725–F730 and its bodies also cite the real ledger findings F710, F713, F714 and F724, so the lane-local pattern and SP5's citation pattern are **the same pattern** and only membership of the lane's own map separates them. The ledger ran to F724, so the assignment from F725 upward in the file's own order is the **identity** and the rewrite is provably a no-op — asserted rather than assumed (`rewrites == 0`), with the out-of-map citations compared **by equality** before and after: measured with `grep -on`, the entry bodies carry `F713` once (F727's heading), `F725` once and `F728` once, all three in F729's body or a heading, and the preamble the parse does not reach carries the rest. The lane's own note is the reason to record it: **a lane that keeps counting from its brief lands on someone else's body, and SP5 checks a citation's existence and not its aim** — its first draft numbered from F714 and collided with six live findings. **Two existing entries were corrected in place, each an asserted replacement with the disproved claim kept visible**: F710's two frames are six and ten pairs (F725), and F713's premise is false outright (F727), so both entries carry a correction paragraph and both rows here carry the correction rather than leaving a falsified claim standing in a second document — F86 and F89's class, which is a claim surviving because only the body was fixed. Measured before at 728 = 728 and after at 735 = 735, from SP6's own counters.

**F87, F88 and F89 are filed and not yet grouped above** — they arrived from the step-3a
partition, which is `CALCIUM_GAP_PLAN.md`. Regrouping is 3b's, with the roadmap rewrite.

**Dispositions were checked against the repository where they move**, not against `FINDINGS.md`'s
prose — that being the record whose authority is in question. Scoped to findings whose
disposition changed and to groups 1–4. Findings in the low-ranked groups carry their filed
disposition unverified, and this sentence is that limit stated rather than left implicit.

`make enforce` resolves every `Fnn` cited above, including F81 and F82 — **306 files, 665
citations, 0 violations, and those numbers are the check.** This file is inside that scope
for the first time, at 175 citations of its own; until F82 it was not, so the sentence this
paragraph makes would have been false had it been written yesterday.

Reading SP5's counters rather than its exit status is what found F82: `enforce` was green, as
it has been through all three of that rule's vacuities, and the number beside the green
disagreed with the tree. The double-count that widening introduced was caught the same way —
`415 + 250 = 665` against a reported `781`, a difference of exactly the ledger's 116
self-citations.

**Which is the frame-read applied to an enforcement rule** — the exit status is the
assertion and the counters are the frame — and it belongs in the list below as much as in
group 11.

## What this moves in `docs/ROADMAP.md`

ROADMAP was drawn from the F55 version and its ranking predates F56–F81. **Named entry by
entry, so step 3 acts on it rather than re-deriving it:**

| ROADMAP entry | at F55 | at F81 | what moved it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** the producer-context contract | 6 | **9** | **F77** makes it structural — asking forces a route change. F13 + F58b are one fact from two sides, and F58b's fix is a *narrower* type where ROADMAP's F58 note asked for a wider one |
| **2** three builder fields, F27's shape | 5 (3 closed) | **12** (7 closed) | F7, F17, F70, F71 are the same shape; **F78** is the sharpest open instance and adds the pair-of-throws problem, which is not a field |
| **3** absence vs failure | 8 | **8** (5 closed) | no count change, but **F67** is the first instance where the framework itself says nothing — no crash, no refusal, no exit — and **F61** is the first where the emptiness is a bug rather than a state |
| **4** a change axis distinct from `Tone` | 3 | **4** | **F81**, and a fourth independent consumer is the threshold this list is ranked on. It also supplies the 1-bit answer twice over: `/diff` shipped text, `/build` shipped a word in a column, neither knowing about the other |
| — | — | **absent** | **three groups with no ROADMAP entry**: the instrument (6), a claim carried without a record (5), a time-based assertion under contention (2) |

**Nothing demotes, and that is the finding about the ranking.** All four moved because a fourth
or fifth consumer turned up, not because anything got more severe — the F55 triage's own
mechanism arriving again, exactly as it should.

**The actionable divergence is that group 9 outranks ROADMAP's entry 4 on consumer count and is
not on the list at all.** A whole group about the apparatus every other finding was measured
with — the size is in the ranking table and is derived, not restated here — and its disposition — *every instrument gets a fixture it must reproduce* — is cheaper
than any of the four entries ROADMAP already carries.

ROADMAP is **not** edited here. Step 3 rewrites it from this file.
